Projects · From the public record

The Holmes–Balwani Texts

Every message in the seven text-message exhibits from Elizabeth Holmes’s 2021 fraud trial, read from the court’s scans and set out as they would look on a phone. The exhibits are in the order the jury saw them, and each conversation opens with a note on what it was offered to show.

1,307 messages · 7 exhibit parts · 195 scanned pages · sent June 2011 – May 2016 · shown at trial Sept.–Nov. 2021

United States v. Holmes, No. 5:18-cr-00258 (N.D. Cal.) · Trial Exhibit 5387

Who is texting

Elizabeth Holmes founded Theranos in 2003 and ran it as chief executive. Her messages are on the right.

Sunny Balwani (Ramesh Balwani) was Theranos’s president and chief operating officer from 2009 to 2016, and Holmes’s partner at the time. His messages are on the left.

Christian Holmes is Elizabeth’s younger brother; he worked at Theranos. One message, in a September 2013 group thread.

Jay Rosan was a physician on Walgreens’ innovation team who championed the Theranos partnership there. One message to Holmes, in October 2015.

Terms in the messages

WAG
Walgreens (its stock ticker)
CTN, nanotainer
Theranos’s small blood-collection tube
FS
fingerstick, a drop of blood from the finger instead of a blood draw from the arm (venipuncture)
CLIA lab
Theranos’s clinical lab, run under federal lab rules (CLIA)
CMS
the federal agency that inspects clinical labs
PSC
patient service center: a Theranos testing site inside a store
PT
proficiency testing, the outside checks a lab must pass
LDT
laboratory-developed test
JC
John Carreyrou of the Wall Street Journal

Exhibit 5387B

The first texts the jury saw

Government exhibit · Sept. 22, 2021 · read page by page through PwC’s Justin Offen

The government’s first excerpt from Exhibit 5387, a 449-page spreadsheet of about 12,000 Holmes–Balwani messages that the accounting firm PwC rebuilt from Theranos’s records. Prosecutors used it to show what Holmes knew about the lab, the money and the Walgreens rollout while she was telling investors otherwise; that she and Balwani acted as partners, the basis of the conspiracy charge; and that they moved against former employees who raised concerns. Messages about the Wall Street Journal were admitted only to show what Holmes knew and when.

293 messages · Nov. 2013 – Oct. 2015 · 30 pages · the court’s scan (PDF)

5387B5387DiMessage

Nov. 19, 2013, 8:54 PM PST

On the first page of the government’s first excerpt (Sept. 22, 2021), which prosecutors offered as evidence of what Holmes knew about problems inside Theranos.

Balwani tells Holmes that chem8 or chem14 should be the first thing monitored when the patch is applied.

Exhibit 5387B, p. 1 · court scan

Tuesday, November 19, 2013

Sunny Balwani

Btw. When we do the patch, chem8 or chem14 is the most important and first thing to monitor
8:54 PM

Printed 11/20/2013 4:54:24 AM UTC · Exhibit 5387B, page 1 of the court’s PDF, row 1 · record Holmes_iPhone_iMessage-MMS-SMS_007317

5387B5387DiMessage

Nov. 20, 2013, 2:14 AM – 2:31 AM PST

Read to the jury on Sept. 22, 2021, the first of the texts read aloud: Balwani complaining that no one was running key production and assay work. Offered to show what Holmes knew about problems inside the company.

Balwani says nobody manages several production and assay areas; Holmes says recruiting is under way and asks about giving Surabh documents before a visit.

Exhibit 5387B, pp. 1–2 · court scan

5387B5387DiMessage

Nov. 27, 2013, 6:03 PM – 6:08 PM PST

Read to the jury on Sept. 22, 2021 and again in the cross-examination of Holmes. The government said it shows she knew Theranos had little cash left while projecting hundreds of millions in revenue to investors.

Balwani reports that the company has $15 million in free cash, and Holmes asks him to stop by to discuss it.

5387B5387DiMessage

Nov. 18, 2014, 8:56 PM – 9:13 PM PST

Read to the jury on Sept. 22, 2021. The government cited “We can’t scale with WAG” as proof she knew the Walgreens rollout was stalling while telling investors otherwise, and the judge noted that these November 2014 texts also bear on why lab director Adam Rosendorff quit. On redirect the defense used them to show Balwani taking charge.

Balwani and Holmes discuss customer-service and lab problems, needed hires, and Walgreens holding off on new centers until July, and want Safeway and CVS.

Exhibit 5387B, pp. 4–6 · court scan

Sunny Balwani

Need to focus on ops. Getting hurt in market.

Exhibit 5387A

Money coming in while the lab director warned

Government exhibit · Sept. 28, 2021 · during former lab director Adam Rosendorff’s testimony

Two pages read while Dr. Adam Rosendorff testified. He had been Theranos’s lab director and had warned that its tests were unreliable. The prosecutor asked whether he knew that, while he doubted the testing, Holmes and Balwani were raising tens of millions from investors. The point for the investor-fraud charges: the money kept coming in while the warnings were being given.

13 messages · Nov. 2014 – Dec. 2014 · 2 pages · the court’s scan (PDF)

5387A5387DiMessage

Nov. 26, 2014, 6:46 PM – 6:49 PM PST

Holmes reports, after finishing calls, that Alice Walton is in for 50, Greg confirmed 100 and Rupert is over 100; Balwani is pleased.

  • Alice Walton: Walmart heiress; the Walton family invested in Theranos
  • Rupert Murdoch: media owner (News Corp, Fox) who invested in Theranos

Exhibit 5387C

The Walgreens partnership

Government exhibit · Oct. 14, 2021 · read by Walgreens executive Nimesh Jhaveri

Sixteen pages read to the jury by Nimesh Jhaveri, the Walgreens executive who worked on the partnership. Walgreens was the deal that made Theranos look real to investors and patients; these texts show the founders’ private view of it, including the October 2015 decision not to tell Walgreens about a change to the blood-collection tube, which the government later called evidence of Holmes’s intent.

Exhibit 5387F

Thanksgiving 2013, and after

Defense exhibit · Nov. 29, 2021 · during Holmes’s own testimony

Offered by the defense near the end of Holmes’s direct testimony. She tied the Thanksgiving 2013 exchange to Balwani’s anger at the time she spent with her family, and said it was followed by the abuse she described. It went to her state of mind: the defense argued that she believed what she told investors and was under his control. Balwani denied her allegations in court filings.

Exhibit 5387G

The love texts, on cross-examination

Government exhibit · Nov. 30, 2021 · at the end of the cross-examination of Holmes

Eight pages that prosecutor Robert Leach put to Holmes at the end of his cross-examination, to answer her account of an abusive, controlling relationship, which Balwani has denied. He put it to her that the full exhibit runs 449 pages and over 12,000 messages and uses “love” more than 594 times; Holmes agreed that she too had been loving and supportive.

224 messages · June 2011 – Oct. 2015 · 8 pages · the court’s scan (PDF)

Notes and sources

About this edition

The case

Holmes was charged with defrauding Theranos’s investors and its patients. In January 2022 the jury convicted her on four counts of defrauding investors and acquitted her on the patient counts; it could not agree on three others. Balwani was tried separately in 2022, where further parts of Exhibit 5387 (5387H and 5387I) were shown; those are not included here.

How this was made

The court posted each exhibit as scanned printouts with no searchable text. Every row was read twice: by an AI model (Anthropic’s Claude) looking at the scan, and by optical character recognition. Where the two disagreed, or the same message read differently in two exhibits, the row was checked again against the scan. Spelling, abbreviations and punctuation are as printed, and Skype’s emoticon codes are shown as the emoticon. A few long messages are cut off in the printout itself; they are labelled.

Times: PwC’s witness testified that the spreadsheet’s timestamps are UTC. They are shown here in Pacific time, where Theranos was based. Select a message’s time to see it as printed, in UTC, and where the row sits in the exhibit.

Black boxes are the court’s redactions; some are white in the scan. Phone numbers in the sender column are also blacked out and are left out here.

The notes on how each exhibit was used were researched and drafted with Claude’s help, from the trial transcripts, the parties’ filings and news reports. Where the record doesn’t say how a particular exchange was used, the note says so. This is an unofficial transcription for reading; the court’s scans are the record.

If you spot a misreading, please let me know; each conversation links to its page of the court’s scan, so a correction can be checked.
